The company has approximately 14–19 employees and an estimated $1–5 million in annual revenue (per third-party business data aggregators). ASP.MD offers an integrated, web-based practice management and EHR platform alongside professional billing and compliance services. The company targets independent medical practices — primarily internal medicine and family medicine — that want to remain independent while managing regulatory complexity. There is no indication of any acquisitions, mergers, or rebranding history; the company appears to have operated under the same name since founding.\n     9→\n    10→## Product: ASP.MD Medical Office System (AMOS)\n    11→\n    12→CHPL ID: 10818\n    13→\n    14→### What It Is\n    15→\n    16→AMOS is a fully web-based, cloud-hosted, integrated Electronic Health Record (EHR) and Practice Management (PM) system. It is browser-based with no client-side software installation required. The product is subscription-based with no upfront licensing costs, and all updates, maintenance, and backups are handled by ASP.MD. The certified module appears to be the entire product — ASP.MD does not describe separate product lines or component-based licensing. This is a single, integrated platform covering clinical documentation, practice management, billing, and patient portal functions.\n    17→\n    18→The CHPL certification is broad, covering 33 criteria across clinical data (a)(1)–(a)(14), transitions of care (b)(1)–(b)(3), clinical quality measures (c)(1), patient portal/VDT (e)(1)/(e)(3), public health reporting (f)(1)/(f)(2), FHIR APIs (g)(7)/(g)(10), and immunization registry (h)(1). The SED intended user description is \"Internal Medicine.\"\n    19→\n    20→### Users & Market\n    21→\n    22→ASP.MD targets small, independent ambulatory medical practices. No customer counts, site numbers, or user counts are published on the vendor's website. The company has approximately 14–19 employees and an estimated $1–5 million in annual revenue (per third-party business data aggregators). ASP.MD offers an integrated, web-based practice management and EHR platform alongside professional billing and compliance services. The company targets independent medical practices — primarily internal medicine and family medicine — that want to remain independent while managing regulatory complexity. There is no indication of any acquisitions, mergers, or rebranding history; the company appears to have operated under the same name since founding.\n\n## Product: ASP.MD Medical Office System (AMOS)\n\nCHPL ID: 10818\n\n### What It Is\n\nAMOS is a fully web-based, cloud-hosted, integrated Electronic Health Record (EHR) and Practice Management (PM) system. It is browser-based with no client-side software installation required. The product is subscription-based with no upfront licensing costs, and all updates, maintenance, and backups are handled by ASP.MD. The certified module appears to be the entire product — ASP.MD does not describe separate product lines or component-based licensing. This is a single, integrated platform covering clinical documentation, practice management, billing, and patient portal functions.\n\nThe CHPL certification is broad, covering 33 criteria across clinical data (a)(1)–(a)(14), transitions of care (b)(1)–(b)(3), clinical quality measures (c)(1), patient portal/VDT (e)(1)/(e)(3), public health reporting (f)(1)/(f)(2), FHIR APIs (g)(7)/(g)(10), and immunization registry (h)(1). The SED intended user description is \"Internal Medicine.\"\n\n### Users & Market\n\nASP.MD targets small, independent ambulatory medical practices. Specialties mentioned on the website include internal medicine, internal medicine subspecialties, family medicine, and \"many other medical specialties.\" The company positions itself as a solution for practices that want to stay independent rather than joining large health systems — providing not just software but also billing services and compliance guidance (MACRA/MIPS).\n\nThe company is very small. No customer counts, site numbers, or user counts are published on the vendor's website. No third-party reviews were found on G2, Capterra, or SoftwareAdvice — the product does not appear to be listed on any of these platforms. Third-party business data suggests roughly 14–19 employees, which is consistent with a vendor serving a small number of practices. There are no notable customer case studies or press releases.\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\nBased on the vendor's website (primarily the software features page and homepage):\n\n**Scheduling & Registration**\n- Appointment scheduling with automated phone/text/email reminders\n- Patient registration\n- Automatic electronic eligibility checking (real-time insurance verification)\n\n**Electronic Health Record**\n- Clinical documentation with templates and checklists\n- Problem lists, medications, allergies tracking\n- Clinical notes and encounter documentation\n- Lab result management with built-in connectivity to external laboratories (LabCorp and Quest mentioned in search results, though not prominently on the current website)\n- Image and document management\n- Clinical decision support\n\n**E-Prescribing**\n- Electronic prescribing module (CHPL certification for (a)(1) CPOE medications confirms this)\n- Drug interaction checking (certified under (a)(4))\n\n**Billing & Claims**\n- Electronic coding with payer-specific rules, eliminating paper encounter forms\n- HCC (Hierarchical Condition Category) optimization alerts\n- Automated claims generation and submission\n- Claims scrubbing and adjudication tracking\n- Electronic remittance/payment posting\n- Reported first-pass claim acceptance rate of 98%+\n- Real-time billing visibility\n\n**Patient Portal**\n- Patient access to core health information\n- Lab results viewing\n- Secure messaging with providers\n- Clinical summary download\n- Setup via email capture at check-in\n\n**Quality Reporting / MIPS**\n- MIPS analytics integrated into the platform\n- ASP.MD is one of only 17 vendors certified as both a CMS data submission vendor and CMS-certified registry\n- Supports 48 certified clinical quality measures (per mandatory disclosures page)\n\n**Public Health Reporting**\n- Immunization registry transmission (certified (h)(1))\n- Syndromic surveillance reporting (certified (f)(2))\n- Transmission to public health agencies (certified (f)(1))\n\n**Transitions of Care**\n- C-CDA generation for transitions of care (certified (b)(1)–(b)(3))\n- Ability to receive and reconcile incoming transition documents\n\n**API Access**\n- FHIR-based API access (certified (g)(7)–(g)(10))\n\n**Professional Services** (offered alongside the software)\n- Medical billing services (ASP.MD appears to offer billing-as-a-service, not just billing software)\n- Medical transcription services\n- MACRA/MIPS compliance guidance and consulting\n\n### Data & Content\n\nBased on the features described above and the certification criteria, AMOS manages:\n\n- **Patient demographics** — registration, insurance eligibility, contact information\n- **Clinical records** — problem lists, medications, allergies, clinical notes, encounter documentation\n- **Orders** — medication orders (e-prescribing), lab orders, imaging/radiology orders (certified (a)(2)/(a)(3))\n- **Results** — lab results (with external lab connectivity), imaging results\n- **Documents and images** — clinical documents, images, scanned records\n- **Scheduling data** — appointments, reminder logs\n- **Billing and claims data** — encounter coding, claims, remittances, payment posting, eligibility checks\n- **Patient portal data** — secure messages between patients and providers, patient-accessed summaries\n- **Quality measure data** — CQM tracking data for 48 measures, MIPS submissions\n- **Public health submissions** — immunization data, syndromic surveillance data\n- **Transition of care documents** — sent and received C-CDAs\n- **Prescriptions** — e-prescribing history, drug interaction checks\n- **Audit logs** — required by certification criteria (d)(1)–(d)(9)\n\nThe vendor's mandatory disclosures confirm there are no additional third-party software licenses required for operation, reinforcing that this is a single integrated system — all data listed above lives within AMOS.\n\n**Gaps in information:** The website does not mention specific details about referral management, fax integration, patient consent tracking, or detailed document management beyond clinical images. It is unclear whether the system stores structured family history data (though (a)(12) family health history is a certified criterion, confirming it does). The website also does not describe any data analytics or population health features beyond MIPS reporting. No information was found about behavioral health, substance abuse, or specialty-specific clinical modules — the product appears to be a general ambulatory EHR/PM without deep specialty customization.\n","numLines":106,"startLine":1,"totalLines":106}}}

in the time of COVID-19:<\\/h1>\\n<p>&nbsp;<\\/p>\\n<p>Due to coronavirus, after 3\\/6\\/2020, CMS will now allow for billing telemedicine visits for patients at home. Commercial payers have followed suit. There are 3 types of visits you can bill:<\\/p>\\n<ol>\\n<li><em><strong>Telehealth visits<\\/strong><\\/em>: Live video chat with your new or established patients. The call can be initiated by you or by the patient. You can bill the office visit codes you are used to billing (99213, etc). You should add a GT modifier to each visit. For medicare, the correct location should be 02 Telemedicine; however, we will make this correction for you as necessary.<\\/li>\\n<li><em><strong>Virtual check in<\\/strong><\\/em>: A patient initiated contact to you in any way. Typically this would be the patient contacting you to find out if they should come in for a visit. You can bill a G2012 if the patient will not come in for a visit within the next 24 hours. Additionally, you can bill a G2010 if the patient sent you images or documentation to review, to determine if they need to come in. Note that you can also initiate contact with the patient, however, they must consent to being evaluated in this way.<\\/li>\\n<li><em><strong>E-Visit:<\\/strong><\\/em> Total time you spend managing a patient via the patient portal within a patient in a 7 day period.\\n<ol>\\n<li>Codes For Physicians:\\n<ol>\\n<li>99421: Online digital evaluation and management service, for an established patient, for up to 7 days, cumulative time during the 7 days; 5\\u201310 minutes<\\/li>\\n<li>99422: Online digital evaluation and management service, for an established patient, for up to 7 days cumulative time during the 7 days; 11\\u2013 20 minutes<\\/li>\\n<li>99423: Online digital evaluation and management service, for an established patient, for up to 7 days, cumulative time during the 7 days; 21 or more minutes.<\\/li>\\n<\\/ol>\\n<\\/li>\\n<li>Code for non Physicians (for example \\u2013 physical therapists, occupational therapists, speech language pathologists, clinical psychologists):\\n<ol>\\n<li>G2061: Qualified non-physician healthcare professional online assessment and management, for an established patient, for up to seven days, cumulative time during the 7 days; 5\\u201310 minutes<\\/li>\\n<li>\\u00a0G2062: Qualified non-physician healthcare professional online assessment and management service, for an established patient, for up to seven days, cumulative time during the 7 days; 11\\u201320 minutes<\\/li>\\n<li>G2063: Qualified non-physician qualified healthcare professional assessment and management service, for an established patient, for up to seven days, cumulative time during the 7 days; 21 or more minutes.<\\/li>\\n<\\/ol>\\n<\\/li>\\n<\\/ol>\\n<\\/li>\\n<li>You may have heard that you must have established relationships with the patients you manage via Virtual Check In or E-Visit; however, CMS will NOT audit for who actually initiated the communications during the coronavirus crisis. You may also have heard that you cannot provide services to patients at home; however, during the crisis, you will be allowed to provide services to patients at home.<\\/li>\\n<li>We recommend having your staff establish the connection to the patient initially, and that they confirm patient billing information (name, address, insurance, guarantor), etc, before passing the patient to you.<\\/li>\\n<li>Additional Information:\\n<ol>\\n<li><strong>Medicare<\\/strong>:\\u00a0<a href=\\\"https:\\/\\/www.cms.gov\\/newsroom\\/fact-sheets\\/medicare-telemedicine-health-care-provider-fact-sheet\\\">https:\\/\\/www.cms.gov\\/newsroom\\/fact-sheets\\/medicare-telemedicine-health-care-provider-fact-sheet<\\/a><\\/li>\\n<li><strong>BCBS<\\/strong>:\\u00a0<a href=\\\"https:\\/\\/provider.bluecrossma.com\\/ProviderHome\\/wcm\\/connect\\/926dbcde-330d-45bc-9fcb-d5ba2e0f78fd\\/Telemedicine_payment_policy.pdf?MOD=AJPERES&amp;CVID=\\\">https:\\/\\/provider.bluecrossma.com\\/ProviderHome\\/wcm\\/connect\\/926dbcde-330d-45bc-9fcb-d5ba2e0f78fd\\/Telemedicine_payment_policy.pdf?MOD=AJPERES&amp;CVID=<\\/a><\\/li>\\n<li><strong>United<\\/strong>:\\u00a0<a href=\\\"https:\\/\\/www.uhcprovider.com\\/content\\/dam\\/provider\\/docs\\/public\\/policies\\/index\\/oxford\\/telemedicine-ohp.pdf\\\">https:\\/\\/www.uhcprovider.com\\/content\\/dam\\/provider\\/docs\\/public\\/policies\\/index\\/oxford\\/telemedicine-ohp.pdf<\\/a><\\/li>\\n<\\/ol>\\n<\\/li>\\n<\\/ol>\\n\",\"protected\":false},\"excerpt\":{\"rendered\":\"<p>&nbsp; &nbsp; Telemedicine in the time of COVID-19: &nbsp; Due to coronavirus, after 3\\/6\\/2020, CMS will now allow for billing telemedicine visits for patients at home. The list goes on&#8230;<\\/p>\\n<p>The regulatory environment for today&#8217;s medical practices is overwhelming. Just when you think things have settled down, congress releases\\u00a0another 2,000 pages of healthcare legislation, including lengthy updates to all of the healthcare IT programs. It&#8217;s simply too much.<\\/p>\\n<p>Unless, of course, you&#8217;re with ASP.MD. ASP.MD simplifies the regulatory burden. We make data gathering and reporting seamless and easy. Our distributed documentation keeps on you top of the reporting requirements. Our system tracks your data and presents it in useful dashboards so that you can see how you&#8217;re doing. ASP.MD even monitors your progress, to see how you&#8217;re doing. And finally, if you need to, you can call and actually talk to an expert. Someone who knows what they&#8217;re doing, and can tell you what to do.<\\/p>\\n<p>Have you been forced into manual registry reporting? Combing your EHR for data, manually extracting it, and then plugging it in to another website!? What&#8217;s the point of having an electronic health record, when you have to manually extract data?<\\/p>\\n<p>Or, are you locked in to claims based reporting, where you have one chance to get it right, and you can&#8217;t go back.<\\/p>\\n<p>Consider the ASP.MD alternative; see your patient, document your visit, and let us take care of the rest. We&#8217;re one of only 17 vendors in the country which is both a CMS &#8220;data submission vendor&#8221; AND a CMS certified &#8220;Registry&#8221;. And unlike other registries, there&#8217;s no manual data entry with ASP.MD.<\\/p>\\n<p>ASP.MD has gotten every one of our providers through meaningful use and PQRS. full_width=&#8221;stretch_row&#8221; full_height=&#8221;yes&#8221; content_placement=&#8221;middle&#8221; parallax=&#8221;content-moving&#8221; css=&#8221;.vc_custom_1496793062113{padding-top: 150px !important;padding-bottom: 150px !important;background-color: #000000 !important;}&#8221;][vc_column][vc_column_text css_animation=&#8221;bottom-to-top&#8221;]<\\/p>\\n<h1>Software that doesn&#8217;t make you want to quit medicine&#8230;<\\/h1>\\n<p>[\\/vc_column_text][vc_column_text css_animation=&#8221;bottom-to-top&#8221; el_class=&#8221;white_text&#8221;]<\\/p>\\n<p style=\\\"text-align: left;\\\">In the last several years, you&#8217;ve probably been forced onto an expensive to buy, expensive to maintain EHR that doesn&#8217;t even do everything you need and is already behind the curve. It may even require you to do extra, redundant work or require you to export data to another system for quality analytics.\\u00a0Meanwhile, you&#8217;re thinking that a software system is supposed to make your life easier, not harder. After all, isn&#8217;t that the point?<\\/p>\\n<p style=\\\"text-align: left;\\\">Enter ASP.MD. Totally integrated, fully web based practice management, EHR, and MIPS analytics without any up front costs. Always up to date without any maintenance, everything you need for MACRA and MIPS, and a whole lot more. Incredibly well designed, ASP.MD minimizes the extra data entry you need to do, by combing through the data it already has on your patients to meet quality measures and eliminate your need to double document. The system is incredibly affordable. And if you use it, it will probably result in increased earnings which outweigh its costs.<\\/p>\\n<p style=\\\"text-align: left;\\\">Perhaps it&#8217;s time to get away from a system you&#8217;ve been forced onto, and instead go with something you choose. You just might feel a whole lot better.<\\/p>\\n<p>[\\/vc_column_text][vc_row_inner css=&#8221;.vc_custom_1453387267885{padding-top: 30px !important;}&#8221;][vc_column_inner width=&#8221;1\\/3&#8243;][vc_icon type=&#8221;linecons&#8221; icon_linecons=&#8221;vc_li vc_li-display&#8221; color=&#8221;custom&#8221; size=&#8221;xl&#8221; align=&#8221;center&#8221; css_animation=&#8221;bottom-to-top&#8221; custom_color=&#8221;#ffffff&#8221;][vc_column_text css_animation=&#8221;bottom-to-top&#8221; css=&#8221;.vc_custom_1493318639523{margin-top: -10px !important;}&#8221;]<\\/p>\\n<h2 style=\\\"text-align: center;\\\">EHR<\\/h2>\\n<p>[\\/vc_column_text][vc_column_text css_animation=&#8221;bottom-to-top&#8221; css=&#8221;.vc_custom_1497882336352{margin-top: -30px !important;}&#8221; el_class=&#8221;white_text&#8221;]The ASP.MD Medical Office System was designed and built by a physician software engineer. He understood better than just about anyone just how busy doctors are, just how unfamiliar with computers many of them are, and, just how arcane a software system can quickly become if not properly designed.<\\/p>\\n<p>So, he built a system focused on usability, and built around several key design features.<\\/p>\\n<ul>\\n<li>Make the system powerful, but easy to use.<\\/li>\\n<li>Limit data entry; don&#8217;t require unnecessary or redundant data entry.<\\/li>\\n<li>Integrate all parts of the system, so that it&#8217;s very easy to move from one section to another, with minimal clicks.<\\/li>\\n<li>Concentrate a lot of data on a few screens, so that a lot of navigation isn&#8217;t required.<\\/li>\\n<li>Build help and tips right in to the workflow, so that users know what&#8217;s what.<\\/li>\\n<li>Provide excellent online documentation to assist users as necessary.<\\/li>\\n<li>Assist users behind the scenes, doing things for them that they would otherwise have to do themselves.<\\/li>\\n<\\/ul>\\n<p>In addition to good design, the Medical Office System is also molded through years of user feedback. Hundreds of features have been added per user requests. And features have been fine tuned and modified until they are truly intuitive. ASP.MD takes customer feedback to heart, and it shows.<\\/p>\\n<p>The result of these principles is\\u00a0a very powerful system, that&#8217;s not hard to use. See the panel below for screen shots and page by page list of features. See our features page for a summary of features.[\\/vc_column_text][\\/vc_column_inner][vc_column_inner width=&#8221;1\\/3&#8243;][vc_icon type=&#8221;linecons&#8221; icon_linecons=&#8221;vc_li vc_li-shop&#8221; color=&#8221;custom&#8221; size=&#8221;xl&#8221; align=&#8221;center&#8221; css_animation=&#8221;bottom-to-top&#8221; custom_color=&#8221;#ffffff&#8221;][vc_column_text css_animation=&#8221;bottom-to-top&#8221; css=&#8221;.vc_custom_1493318666806{margin-top: -10px !important;}&#8221;]<\\/p>\\n<h2 style=\\\"text-align: center;\\\">PRACTICE MANAGEMENT<\\/h2>\\n<p>[\\/vc_column_text][vc_column_text css_animation=&#8221;bottom-to-top&#8221; css=&#8221;.vc_custom_1497882381807{margin-top: -30px !important;}&#8221; el_class=&#8221;white_text&#8221;]A good practice management system is as important to your practice as a good EHR. The ASP.MD medical office system features powerful scheduling and registration, with a host of added features like automatic electronic eligibility checking and an automated phone\\/text\\/email appointment reminder system. AMOS makes sure that your staff captures everything that&#8217;s necessary for billing on patient check in. Electronic coding eliminates paper encounter forms; built in coding rules reduce coding errors. HCC tips alert you to the best diagnoses to choose for maximal reimbursement. All this adds up to clean, ready to submit claims which are then automatically submitted by the system, with a first pass rate of over 98%.<\\/p>\\n<p>Following submission, claims are scrubbed and then adjudicated, and any rejected claims immediately flagged for follow up. Likewise, accepted claims are paid and electronically posted, typically within 15 days.<\\/p>\\n<p>Finally and perhaps best of all, the ASP.MD Practice Management components give you complete insight in to your billing status, in real time, at any time. If you elect to have ASP.MD work your claims, you can see how we&#8217;re doing, right down to the claim level, whenever you want. No more putting blind faith in your billing company, and finding out months after it&#8217;s too late that they&#8217;ve let tens or even hundreds of thousands go. Take a look at the panels below for an up close look at ASP.MD Practice Management features.[\\/vc_column_text][\\/vc_column_inner][vc_column_inner width=&#8221;1\\/3&#8243;][vc_icon type=&#8221;linecons&#8221; icon_linecons=&#8221;vc_li vc_li-user&#8221; color=&#8221;white&#8221; size=&#8221;xl&#8221; align=&#8221;center&#8221; css_animation=&#8221;bottom-to-top&#8221;][vc_column_text css_animation=&#8221;bottom-to-top&#8221; css=&#8221;.vc_custom_1494191128211{margin-top: -10px !important;}&#8221;]<\\/p>\\n<h2 style=\\\"text-align: center;\\\">PATIENT PORTAL<\\/h2>\\n<p>[\\/vc_column_text][vc_column_text css_animation=&#8221;bottom-to-top&#8221; css=&#8221;.vc_custom_1497882422498{margin-top: -30px !important;}&#8221; el_class=&#8221;white_text&#8221;]The ASP.MD Patient Portal makes communication with your patients easy. To add a patient to the portal, simply get their email address at the front desk. Then, click a button, and ASP.MD will email the patient a login to the portal and instructions on how to get going. From there, it&#8217;s as easy as can be; the patient logs in, and they can see all of their core\\u00a0information, see lab results, and message you or receive a message from you. You can also, at your option, easily share notes or documents with the patient. Finally, they can download a summary of their clinical information for another provider should they need it. See the panel below for images of the patient portal, what it features, and how easy it is to use it.[\\/vc_column_text][\\/vc_column_inner][\\/vc_row_inner][\\/vc_column][\\/vc_row]<\\/p>\\n\",\"protected\":false},\"excerpt\":{\"rendered\":\"<p>[vc_row full_width=&#8221;stretch_row&#8221; There is:\n- **No data dictionary** — no tables, fields, columns, data types, or schemas\n- **No export format specification** beyond naming three formats (C-CDA, text, PDF)\n- **No field-level documentation** of any kind\n- **No sample exports or examples**\n- **No schema files** (XSD, JSON Schema, etc.)\n- **No API documentation**\n- **No instructions** for how a practice administrator or patient would initiate an export\n- **No description of what data is included** in the export beyond the implicit scope of C-CDA\n\nThe page was published 2023-11-14 (roughly a year and a half after the product's 2022-02-03 certification date) and has not been modified since.\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nThe export documentation describes three output formats — C-CDA, text files, and PDF files — but provides **no specification of what data domains are included** in any of them.\n\n**C-CDA coverage (implied):** C-CDA is a well-defined standard that covers a specific set of clinical data: demographics, problems, medications, allergies, lab results, vitals, immunizations, procedures, and clinical notes. However, the documentation does not specify which C-CDA document type(s) are generated (CCD, Referral Note, Discharge Summary, etc.) or which sections are populated. It only links to the HL7 Companion Guide — an external reference document, not a vendor-specific mapping.\n\n**Text and PDF files (undefined):** The documentation says the export includes \"text files (industry standard)\" and \"PDF files (industry standard)\" but provides no information about what data these contain, what \"industry standard\" means in this context, or how they relate to the C-CDA content. These could be anything from clinical notes to billing statements to the full medical record — there is no way to know from the documentation.\n\n**Data domains from product research — coverage status:**\n\n| Domain | Covered? | Notes |\n|--------|----------|-------|\n| Demographics | Unknown | Likely in C-CDA but not documented |\n| Problem lists | Unknown | Likely in C-CDA but not documented |\n| Medications / e-prescribing | Unknown | Likely in C-CDA but not documented |\n| Allergies | Unknown | Likely in C-CDA but not documented |\n| Lab results | Unknown | Likely in C-CDA but not documented |\n| Vital signs | Unknown | Likely in C-CDA but not documented |\n| Clinical notes | Unknown | May be in text/PDF files but not documented |\n| Immunizations | Unknown | Likely in C-CDA but not documented |\n| Procedures | Unknown | Likely in C-CDA but not documented |\n| Documents/images | Unknown | May be in PDF files but not documented |\n| **Billing/claims data** | **Not mentioned** | No reference to billing, claims, charges, payments, or remittances |\n| **Scheduling/appointments** | **Not mentioned** | Not required EHI, but notable given it's a PM system |\n| **Insurance/eligibility** | **Not mentioned** | Part of billing records, no reference |\n| **Patient portal messages** | **Not mentioned** | No reference to secure messages |\n| **Quality measure data** | **Not mentioned** | No reference to CQM/MIPS data |\n| **Family health history** | Unknown | May be in C-CDA but not documented |\n| **Implantable devices** | Unknown | May be in C-CDA but not documented |\n| **Care plans** | Unknown | May be in C-CDA but not documented |\n\nThe fundamental problem is that the documentation is too sparse to assess coverage. We can infer that C-CDA will carry the standard clinical data domains, but:\n\n1. There is no confirmation of which C-CDA sections are populated\n2. The text and PDF file contents are completely unspecified\n3. **Billing data — a core data domain for this integrated EHR/PM product — is entirely absent from the documentation.** ASP.MD's product research shows it is a deeply integrated billing system with claims submission, adjudication, electronic remittance, and HCC coding. None of this appears in the export documentation.\n4. No specialty-specific or custom clinical data is described\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\nThe export uses three formats:\n- **C-CDA**: A recognized healthcare data standard, appropriate for clinical summaries. However, C-CDA has inherent limitations — it does not naturally represent billing data, scheduling data, or many types of administrative records. If the export relies solely on C-CDA for structured data, it will miss significant portions of what this integrated EHR/PM system stores.\n- **Text files**: Completely unspecified. No format definition, no field descriptions, no encoding information.\n- **PDF files**: Completely unspecified. These could be rendered clinical notes, printed reports, scanned documents, or anything else.\n\nThe folder structure (`LASTNAME_FIRSTNAME_DOB_MRN`) is the only structural detail provided. It tells us the export is organized per-patient with the MRN linking to the C-CDA content.\n\nA third party receiving this export would have C-CDA data they could parse (using the standard), but the text and PDF files would be opaque without additional documentation. There is no way to programmatically process the non-C-CDA portions of the export.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\nThis is among the most minimal EHI export documentation possible. Five sentences on a web page, with no:\n- Data dictionary\n- Field definitions\n- Data type specifications\n- Value sets or coded value references\n- Sample exports\n- Worked examples\n- User instructions for initiating an export\n- Schema or machine-readable format specification\n- Version history or change log\n\nA developer could not implement an import of this data based on the documentation alone. They would know to expect C-CDA files (parseable via the standard) in patient-named folders, but the text and PDF files would require manual inspection of actual export output to understand.\n\nThe documentation reads as a compliance checkbox — the minimum text needed to have a page at the registered URL. It was published in November 2023, roughly 20 months after certification, and has not been updated since.\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n- **Granularity**: Page-level only. No tables, no fields, no data types.\n- **Coded fields**: Not documented.\n- **Relationships**: Not documented (the MRN linking to C-CDA is the only cross-reference mentioned).\n- **Versioning**: None.\n\n### (b)(10) vs (g)(10) Assessment\n\nThe documentation does not appear to conflate (b)(10) with (g)(10). It does not mention FHIR, Bulk Data, or standardized APIs. However, the reliance on C-CDA as the primary structured format suggests the export may only cover the clinical data domains that C-CDA naturally represents — which substantially overlaps with USCDI/US Core scope. The text and PDF files could extend coverage beyond C-CDA's scope, but without documentation, this is speculative.\n\nThe critical question for this vendor: **does the export include billing data?** ASP.MD is an integrated EHR and billing system. Billing records — claims, charges, payments, remittances, coding — are core EHI for this product. C-CDA does not naturally carry this data. The text and PDF files might contain billing information, but the documentation does not say so.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL (after redirects): https://www.asp.md/export/\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no (content is static HTML, no JS needed)\n- Navigation complexity: direct_link\n- Anti-bot issues: none\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n\nNo technical obstacles were encountered. The URL works, the page loads, the content is accessible. The obstacle is the content itself — it is extremely sparse and does not constitute meaningful technical documentation of the EHI export.\n\n- No downloadable files exist on the page (only an external HL7 link)\n- No additional export documentation exists elsewhere on the site (confirmed via sitemap, WP API, path probing, and web search)\n- The mandatory disclosures page confirms (b)(10) certification but adds no export documentation\n- The software features page describes the product's capabilities but provides no technical export documentation\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":325,"cache_read_input_tokens":57193,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":325},"output_tokens":25,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"40118efb-451d-41d3-a9f0-c2c5cb96c1d3","uuid":"c623e1c4-d7a8-4378-bbe4-c9634045d2c8"}

No specialty-specific or custom clinical data is described\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\nThe export uses three formats:\n- **C-CDA**: A recognized healthcare data standard, appropriate for clinical summaries. However, C-CDA has inherent limitations — it does not naturally represent billing data, scheduling data, or many types of administrative records. If the export relies solely on C-CDA for structured data, it will miss significant portions of what this integrated EHR/PM system stores.\n- **Text files**: Completely unspecified. No format definition, no field descriptions, no encoding information.\n- **PDF files**: Completely unspecified. These could be rendered clinical notes, printed reports, scanned documents, or anything else.\n\nThe folder structure (`LASTNAME_FIRSTNAME_DOB_MRN`) is the only structural detail provided. It tells us the export is organized per-patient with the MRN linking to the C-CDA content.\n\nA third party receiving this export would have C-CDA data they could parse (using the standard), but the text and PDF files would be opaque without additional documentation. There is no way to programmatically process the non-C-CDA portions of the export.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\nThis is among the most minimal EHI export documentation possible. Five sentences on a web page, with no:\n- Data dictionary\n- Field definitions\n- Data type specifications\n- Value sets or coded value references\n- Sample exports\n- Worked examples\n- User instructions for initiating an export\n- Schema or machine-readable format specification\n- Version history or change log\n\nA developer could not implement an import of this data based on the documentation alone. They would know to expect C-CDA files (parseable via the standard) in patient-named folders, but the text and PDF files would require manual inspection of actual export output to understand.\n\nThe documentation reads as a compliance checkbox — the minimum text needed to have a page at the registered URL. It was published in November 2023, roughly 20 months after certification, and has not been updated since.\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n- **Granularity**: Page-level only. No tables, no fields, no data types.\n- **Coded fields**: Not documented.\n- **Relationships**: Not documented (the MRN linking to C-CDA is the only cross-reference mentioned).\n- **Versioning**: None.\n\n### (b)(10) vs (g)(10) Assessment\n\nThe documentation does not appear to conflate (b)(10) with (g)(10). It does not mention FHIR, Bulk Data, or standardized APIs. However, the reliance on C-CDA as the primary structured format suggests the export may only cover the clinical data domains that C-CDA naturally represents — which substantially overlaps with USCDI/US Core scope. The text and PDF files could extend coverage beyond C-CDA's scope, but without documentation, this is speculative.\n\nThe critical question for this vendor: **does the export include billing data?** ASP.MD is an integrated EHR and billing system. Billing records — claims, charges, payments, remittances, coding — are core EHI for this product. C-CDA does not naturally carry this data.
